# Comprehensive Literature Atlas and Environmental Histories of the MAUVE Virgo Galaxy Sample: Mechanisms, Timelines, and gFMR/FMR Implications

**Version:** 2026-08-12  
**Scope:** 39 MAUVE target identifiers, representing 40 physical galaxies  
**Language:** English  
**Document type:** internal PhD-level research monograph and evidence atlas

> **AI disclosure.** This atlas was produced with AI-assisted literature search, metadata reconciliation, evidence extraction, synthesis, figure generation and report drafting. Claims were checked against the cited source records and accessible full texts, but the document still requires domain-expert human review before publication or use as authoritative orbital history.

## Abstract

The MAUVE stage labels compress a heterogeneous set of Virgo histories into pre-peak, near-peak and post-peak bins. This atlas restores the lost mechanism and time information for every target. It combines the live MAUVE catalogue and sample document with a 19,465-link SIMBAD bibliography snapshot, a curated registry of 78 environmentally informative works, and 259 paper--galaxy evidence records. The sample contains 39 target systems and 40 physical components because NGC4567_8 is treated as one observing target but two interacting galaxies. The adopted physical-galaxy counts are 12 pre-peak, 10 near-peak and 18 post-peak; target-system counts are 11, 10 and 18. The dominant result is that stage is not mechanism: secure ram-pressure cases coexist with tides, mergers, direct disk--disk collision, ancient accretion, starvation/old depletion, nuclear or stellar feedback, and ambiguous projected-phase-space assignments. NGC4192 and NGC4698 receive explicit reclassification audits. Each chapter separates observations from models and atlas inference, states timing limitations, and proposes MUSE/CO/H I tests relevant to resolved metallicity--SFR and gas-FMR work.

## How to use this atlas

Use the adopted stage as a sample-design coordinate, not as a literal orbit. For any scientific interpretation, read the chapter's mechanism, timing and contradiction fields together. The evidence grade describes source coverage (tailored model, direct resolved observation, survey measurement or contextual inference); it is not the same as confidence in the reconstructed history. Every gFMR/FMR paragraph labelled **new atlas inference** is a synthesis proposed here, not a claim made by all cited authors.

## Contents

1. Executive synthesis
2. Sample provenance and selection
3. Research and evidence protocol
4. Environmental and gFMR/FMR framework
5. Thirty-nine target chapters
6. Cross-sample synthesis
7. Prioritized follow-up programme
8. Appendices and source registry

# 1. Executive synthesis

## 1.1 Sample accounting

| Counting unit | Pre-peak | Near-peak | Post-peak | Total |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| MAUVE target systems | 11 | 10 | 18 | 39 |
| Physical galaxies | 12 | 10 | 18 | 40 |

“Near-peak” is used throughout. “Close-to-peak” is a synonym appearing in earlier discussion. NGC4567_8 has one system chapter and two component evidence records; its two galaxies must not be merged when computing gas mass, metallicity, SFE or resolved relations.

## 1.2 Principal scientific conclusions

- **Secure recent/active ram pressure:** NGC4330, NGC4388, NGC4402, NGC4501, NGC4522 and NGC4569 have the strongest combinations of resolved gas morphology, stellar-population clocks, radio/X-ray constraints and/or tailored models.
- **Compound gravity plus ICM:** NGC4254, NGC4424 and NGC4654 require tidal/merger histories in addition to ram pressure. NGC4694 is primarily tidal; counting its bridge as a wind tail is misleading.
- **Direct galaxy collision:** NGC4567 and NGC4568 are an early disk--disk collision with a resolved molecular front but no secure absolute encounter clock and no strong present ICM-stripping diagnosis.
- **Ancient accretion is not a stripping clock:** NGC4698's polar nuclear component is older than 5 Gyr; it does not validate the adopted post-peak code. NGC4216's spectacular dwarf disruption proves gravitational processing but leaves the ICM epoch uncertain.
- **Feedback can masquerade as environment:** NGC4064, NGC4383, NGC4424 and NGC4569 contain ionized outflows or AGN-illuminated structures that must be separated from stripped gas and H II-region emission.
- **Chemical inference is path dependent:** gas removal, compression, phase conversion, inflow, mixing and feedback change gas mass, SFR and metallicity on different clocks. A cross-sectional fixed-Mstar FMR/gFMR is not the trajectory of one galaxy.

## 1.4 Reclassification audits

**NGC4192.** Yoon class IV would map mechanically to post-peak, but MAUVE adopts pre-peak because the live sample document reports no H I deficiency or clear stripping. VIVA shows no severe classic truncation, while Köppen classifies it as a low-deficiency active stripper. The defensible reading is not “known first infall”; it is “no secure strong post-peak signature.” The code is retained with moderate-low orbital confidence.

**NGC4698.** Yoon class I and VIVA indicate weak/incipient processing and projection ambiguity, whereas Köppen's analytical model labels it a past stripper. The extended, mostly regular H I disk is unlike a secure post-stripping remnant. Its polar central structure proves an unrelated >5 Gyr accretion history. MAUVE's post-peak code is retained for provenance, but its ICM chronology is explicitly low confidence.

# 2. Sample provenance and selection

## 2.1 Live inputs

The primary property table is [mauve_master_wiki_newclass.dat](/Users/Igniz/Desktop/ICRAR/further/mauve_master_wiki_newclass.dat). Survey-design and stage provenance are taken from [MAUVE_sample.pdf](/Users/Igniz/Desktop/ICRAR/MAUVE/MAUVE_sample.pdf). Galaxy colours are read from [MAUVE_galaxy_colors.dat](/Users/Igniz/Desktop/ICRAR/further/MAUVE_galaxy_colors.dat), specifically the persisted `MAUVE_GALAXY_GLASBEY_PACKAGE_40`; black is reserved for pooled references.

The sample document traces MAUVE through VERTICO and VIVA. The underlying bright-disk selection was modified by H I/CO availability, mass cuts, environmental relevance and MUSE scheduling. MAUVE is therefore environmentally selected and not statistically complete. Mechanism counts in this atlas describe the sample, not unbiased Virgo frequencies.

## 2.2 Catalogue columns and caveats

The live table supplies component name, log stellar mass, log SFR, log H I mass, log H2 mass, `DMS`, line-of-sight velocity and class. Values are reproduced, not recalibrated here. `DMS` is retained with the catalogue's label because its exact formula is not encoded in the file header; it is not used as an orbital diagnostic. Gas masses have tracer/conversion-systematic uncertainties not represented by the printed precision. A single Virgo distance is convenient for sample context but substructure depth matters for physical radii and orbits.

## 2.3 Stage provenance

The sample document maps Yoon classes 0/I to pre-peak, II to near-peak and III/IV to post-peak, except NGC4192 and NGC4698. The bins were designed to order environmental perturbation statistically. They should not be converted into exact times or assumed to mean ram pressure in every object.

![Figure 4. Projected Virgo sky context and major substructure landmarks.](/Users/Igniz/Desktop/ICRAR/MAUVE/assets/20260812_mauve_environmental_atlas/figure_04_sky_subcluster_context.png)

# 3. Research and evidence protocol

## 3.1 Search architecture and saturation

SIMBAD resolved all 40 physical components and returned 1,328 identifier rows. The complete execution-date snapshot contains 19,465 object--bibliography links and 5,213 unique bibcodes. It is preserved as [simbad_all_mentions_20260812.csv](/Users/Igniz/Desktop/ICRAR/MAUVE/assets/20260812_mauve_environmental_atlas/simbad_all_mentions_20260812.csv). The inventory includes catalogue-level appearances; its four title/abstract categories are triage, not evidence grades.

The curated environmental corpus began with VIVA, Yoon, Vollmer and Köppen, then expanded through VERTICO, VESTIGE, GUViCS, Halpha, CO, radio continuum/polarization, X-ray, stellar-population and dynamical studies. Two citation-chain passes were applied across the material corpus, followed by a component-alias/keyword pass. Journal/preprint duplicates were merged as one work. The stopping criterion was no new *material* source in the last chain/alias pass; this is an operational database snapshot through 2026-08-12, not universal completeness.

The full reproducible record is [query_log.md](/Users/Igniz/Desktop/ICRAR/MAUVE/assets/20260812_mauve_environmental_atlas/query_log.md) and the DOI audit is [doi_resolution_audit.json](/Users/Igniz/Desktop/ICRAR/MAUVE/assets/20260812_mauve_environmental_atlas/doi_resolution_audit.json).

## 3.2 Evidence hierarchy

1. **Tailored multiwavelength modelling:** object-specific simulations or SFHs constrained by several tracers. Strong for mechanisms/timing within model assumptions; not automatically a statistical error measurement.
2. **Direct resolved observations:** H I/CO/Halpha/UV/radio/X-ray/stellar morphology or kinematics that directly expose truncation, tails, bridges, compression, shocks or decoupled components.
3. **Survey-level measurement:** resolved or integrated measurements without a unique object-specific mechanism reconstruction.
4. **Phase-space/contextual inference:** projected radius/velocity, subcluster context or analytical pressure/deficiency classifications. Valuable statistically; intrinsically degenerate for individual orbits.

Every evidence record also labels claim type (observed, model-derived, inferred or speculative), locator, access, uncertainty and relevance. Source strength and final-history confidence are separate fields.

## 3.3 Adversarial quality checkpoints

- **Scope checkpoint:** “all papers” is not literally provable. The mitigation is a complete SIMBAD-linked snapshot plus a smaller, fully synthesized environmental corpus; inaccessible or incidental items never support detailed claims.
- **Causal checkpoint:** asymmetric gas is not automatically RPS; bridges, stellar disturbance, AGN cones, starburst winds and projection were tested as alternatives.
- **Timing checkpoint:** quenching lookback, encounter age, tail survival and time from peak pressure are kept distinct. Phase space is never treated as a unique orbit.
- **Selection checkpoint:** MAUVE's mechanism mix is not presented as a Virgo population fraction.
- **Ethics/integrity checkpoint:** no human subjects or dual-use concerns arise; the material risk is citation or causal overstatement, addressed through visible locators, access labels, DOI metadata checks and AI disclosure.

![Figure 5. Evidence coverage by tracer. Grade and final-history confidence are deliberately separate.](/Users/Igniz/Desktop/ICRAR/MAUVE/assets/20260812_mauve_environmental_atlas/figure_05_evidence_coverage_heatmap.png)

*Figure 5. Evidence coverage by tracer. Grade and final-history confidence are deliberately separate.*

# 4. Environmental and gFMR/FMR framework

## 4.1 What the three stages retain—and discard

VIVA provides resolved H I morphology; Yoon combines that morphology with projected phase space; Vollmer orders five tailored RPS models; Köppen compares present and time-integrated analytical pressures. These are complementary rather than interchangeable. A Yoon class is a morphology/context category, a Köppen label is model inference, and a Vollmer time is an object-specific model state.

Projected phase space uses only sky position and line-of-sight velocity. Here, projected radius is recomputed about M87 with D=16.5 Mpc and R200=1.55 Mpc, and velocity is normalized by vcl=1088 km/s and sigma=593 km/s, matching the adopted Yoon/VIVA context. Unknown line-of-sight distance and transverse velocity make multiple orbits compatible with one point.

![Figure 3. Projected phase space. This is statistical context, not a unique reconstruction of any orbit.](/Users/Igniz/Desktop/ICRAR/MAUVE/assets/20260812_mauve_environmental_atlas/figure_03_projected_phase_space.png)

*Figure 3. Projected phase space. This is statistical context, not a unique reconstruction of any orbit.*

## 4.2 Mechanism vocabulary

**Ram pressure** preferentially removes diffuse outer gas, can compress the leading edge, and may permit fallback/re-accretion. **Tides, harassment and mergers** disturb stars as well as gas, produce bridges/streams, and can drive inflow or make gas easier to strip. **Preprocessing** requires evidence that a group or companion acted before/while entering the cluster; projected proximity alone is insufficient. **Starvation** is slow loss of replenishment and is difficult to distinguish from an old weak event in a relaxed disk. **Feedback** can launch ionized/radio/X-ray structures that resemble environmental tails but has a different origin.

![Figure 6. Mechanism-aware synthesis versus the adopted three-stage code.](/Users/Igniz/Desktop/ICRAR/MAUVE/assets/20260812_mauve_environmental_atlas/figure_06_mechanism_stage_matrix.png)

*Figure 6. Mechanism-aware synthesis versus the adopted three-stage code.*

![Figure 7. Published dynamical and stellar-population clocks, shown in separate panels because they date different physical events.](/Users/Igniz/Desktop/ICRAR/MAUVE/assets/20260812_mauve_environmental_atlas/figure_07_processing_timelines.png)

*Figure 7. Published dynamical and stellar-population clocks, shown in separate panels because they date different physical events.*

## 4.3 FMR and gas-FMR interpretation

The classical FMR is a population-level relation among stellar mass, SFR and gas-phase metallicity; gas-based extensions replace or augment SFR with atomic/molecular gas. The current Wang et al. framework ([arXiv:2608.04784](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04784), v1 preprint, not peer reviewed as of 2026-08-12) argues that gas mass is more fundamental and that SFR can enter through SFE. Huang et al. ([DOI](https://doi.org/10.1093/mnras/stag1019)) show within MAUVE that resolved metallicity--SFR residuals are calibration- and galaxy-dependent. Therefore a fixed-Mstar population surface cannot be read as the time track of an individual environmentally processed disk.

Removal changes Mgas before or after SFR depending on phase and density; compression can increase SFR without immediate gas loss; tidal inflow can dilute central metallicity while raising molecular fraction; strangulation can increase metallicity as dilution stops; outflows can export gas and metals selectively. Integrated and resolved measurements can consequently move in different directions.

![Figure 8. Environmental pathways through FMR/gFMR observable space; a population relation is not an evolutionary track.](/Users/Igniz/Desktop/ICRAR/MAUVE/assets/20260812_mauve_environmental_atlas/figure_08_gfmr_environmental_pathways.png)

*Figure 8. Environmental pathways through FMR/gFMR observable space; a population relation is not an evolutionary track.*

# 5. Individual target environmental histories

Each target chapter is an evidence-constrained synthesis. Properties come from the live MAUVE table. Paper-level records below include source locators and access metadata. For galaxies without a dedicated modern environmental study, the chapter says so explicitly rather than promoting survey context into a tailored history.

## 5.1 IC3392

### Identity, catalogue properties and adopted status

| Component | Preferred aliases | log Mstar | log SFR | log MHI | log MH2 | DMS |
|---|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| IC3392 | VCC 1126; UGC 7602; IC 3392 | 9.82 | -0.99 | 7.66 | 8.23 | -0.76 |

| Component | v (km/s) | MAUVE stage | Yoon | Köppen |
|---|---:|---|---:|---|
| IC3392 | 1678 | post-peak | III | past |

### IC3392: observational record and environmental sequence

**Adopted mechanism.** past ram pressure stripping. Secondary/contextual process: none clearly established.

**H I evidence (direct morphology first).** The H I disk is severely, nearly symmetrically truncated while the stellar disk remains regular.

**CO/H2, ionized gas/UV, stars/dust, radio, X-ray and dynamics.** Outer-disk stars record cessation of star formation; no dedicated gravitational alternative is established.

**Best-supported event sequence.** A strong ICM event removed outer gas, after which the remaining disk relaxed.

**Published timing and its meaning.** Outer-disk quenching is about 500+/-100 Myr.

**Competing interpretations and classification tension.** Yoon III and Köppen past agree; the stellar clock is local and does not uniquely reconstruct the orbit.

**History confidence.** high.

**gFMR/FMR implication — new atlas inference.** The system anchors a clean post-stripping path in which low gas fraction and radial censorship precede continued central evolution.

**Specific MAUVE-MUSE/VERTICO test.** Measure the abundance step/gradient across the truncation radius and compare central molecular SFE to other post-peak disks.

## 5.2 NGC4064

### Identity, catalogue properties and adopted status

| Component | Preferred aliases | log Mstar | log SFR | log MHI | log MH2 | DMS |
|---|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| NGC4064 | UGC 7054; NGC 4064 | 10.05 | -0.49 | 7.63 | 8.27 | -0.41 |

| Component | v (km/s) | MAUVE stage | Yoon | Köppen |
|---|---:|---|---:|---|
| NGC4064 | 1000 | post-peak | III | past |

### NGC4064: observational record and environmental sequence

**Adopted mechanism.** past ram pressure plus gravitational disturbance. Secondary/contextual process: star-formation-driven outflow.

**H I evidence (direct morphology first).** H I is confined to the inner few kiloparsecs and strongly deficient, while the outer stellar body is much less truncated.

**CO/H2, ionized gas/UV, stars/dust, radio, X-ray and dynamics.** Non-circular gas and central star formation indicate a gravitational perturbation. MAUVE-MUSE now directly resolves a bipolar ionized outflow with mass loading about 1.66 from the remaining central gas.

**Best-supported event sequence.** A past encounter/stripping episode removed the outer gas and concentrated what survived; approximately 0.4 Gyr later, residual central star formation is driving a wind that may accelerate final quenching.

**Published timing and its meaning.** Outer-disk stellar quenching is about 425+/-75 Myr. The outflow depletion argument is current and uncertain in electron density/geometry; much outflowing gas may remain bound.

**Competing interpretations and classification tension.** RPS alone does not explain all non-circular/stellar features. Feedback is a late-stage mechanism, not evidence that the original stripping was feedback-driven.

**History confidence.** high for past processing and current outflow; moderate for mechanism ordering.

**gFMR/FMR implication — new atlas inference.** This is a two-step path: environmental gas loss changes gas fraction, then feedback changes SFR and observable ionized gas. It can depart from equilibrium gFMR expectations in more than one direction.

**Specific MAUVE-MUSE/VERTICO test.** Measure outflow metallicity and escape fraction, central CO depletion, and stellar-age asymmetry to separate the prior gravitational and hydrodynamic events.

### NGC4189: observational record and environmental sequence

**Dedicated-literature status:** No dedicated modern environmental-processing paper was located in the saturated curated search. The history below is constrained by VIVA/phase-space/analytical and multi-survey evidence; confidence is correspondingly limited.

**Adopted mechanism.** weak hydrodynamic perturbation or group tide. Secondary/contextual process: internal warp.

**H I evidence (direct morphology first).** The H I disk extends slightly beyond the optical disk and is broadly symmetric but warped; the southeastern side has locally enhanced H I.

**CO/H2, ionized gas/UV, stars/dust, radio, X-ray and dynamics.** Halpha and radio emission are also enhanced to the southeast. VIVA notes its likely M-cloud/background location and nearby dwarf companions, so neither ICM compression nor a tidal origin is uniquely selected.

**Best-supported event sequence.** A largely gas-normal disk was mildly disturbed; no evidence establishes a completed stripping passage.

**Published timing and its meaning.** No published pericentric or quenching clock was found; only a present-day weak-disturbance state is defensible.

**Competing interpretations and classification tension.** Yoon class 0 and the absence of a Köppen entry agree that a strong stripping history is unproven, but do not distinguish tide, warp, or weak ram pressure.

**History confidence.** low-moderate.

**gFMR/FMR implication — new atlas inference.** Any metallicity-SFR offset should be interpreted as a possible local gas-supply or compression effect, not as a calibrated infall-time sequence.

**Specific MAUVE-MUSE/VERTICO test.** Use MUSE gas-star kinematic residuals and outer-disk abundance asymmetry; compare the southeastern sector to the opposite side and to resolved CO.

### NGC4192: observational record and environmental sequence

**Dedicated-literature status:** No dedicated modern environmental-processing paper was located in the saturated curated search. The history below is constrained by VIVA/phase-space/analytical and multi-survey evidence; confidence is correspondingly limited.

**Adopted mechanism.** weak or incipient environmental processing. Secondary/contextual process: bar/warp and projection.

**H I evidence (direct morphology first).** M98 has an extended, asymmetric southeastern H I distribution and a warp but is not strongly H I deficient in the adopted catalogue context.

**CO/H2, ionized gas/UV, stars/dust, radio, X-ray and dynamics.** The central gas field is bar-distorted. MAUVE-MUSE finds resolved ionized-gas/metallicity structure, but these data do not by themselves prove a stripping epoch.

**Best-supported event sequence.** An intrinsically warped barred disk may be experiencing low-level pressure; a severe completed event is not required by its current gas reservoir.

**Published timing and its meaning.** No tailored clock. Köppen labels a low-deficiency active case, whereas Yoon class IV is morphology-based and MAUVE adopts pre-peak.

**Competing interpretations and classification tension.** This is a formal reclassification audit: Yoon IV would normally map to a slow/post category, while Köppen places it among low-deficiency active strippers. The retained pre-peak label records the lack of strong present truncation, not certainty about first infall.

**History confidence.** moderate for weak processing; low for orbital stage.

**gFMR/FMR implication — new atlas inference.** Because H I remains abundant, gas mass may still track inflow-like dilution more closely than SFR; bar-driven SFE changes and environmental gas removal are confounded.

**Specific MAUVE-MUSE/VERTICO test.** Measure leading/trailing metallicity and DIG asymmetries; combine MUSE with H I/CO velocity residuals before assigning a direction of motion.

## 5.5 NGC4216

| Component | v (km/s) | MAUVE stage | Yoon | Köppen |
|---|---:|---|---:|---|
| NGC4216 | 30 | post-peak | IV | past |

### NGC4216: observational record and environmental sequence

**Adopted mechanism.** long-term gas depletion plus satellite accretion. Secondary/contextual process: possible preprocessing/slow ICM process.

**H I evidence (direct morphology first).** The H I disk is fairly regular but low surface density and deficient, without a clear current stripping edge.

**CO/H2, ionized gas/UV, stars/dust, radio, X-ray and dynamics.** Deep optical imaging to about 29 mag arcsec^-2 reveals multiple streams, loops and disrupting dwarfs, directly proving ongoing gravitational bombardment; no extended group X-ray halo is detected.

**Best-supported event sequence.** Slow/older gas depletion and repeated minor accretion coexist; the streams need not have caused the H I deficiency, and an ICM or viscous-stripping history remains plausible.

**Published timing and its meaning.** No unique pressure clock. Stream survival is probably shorter than about 1 Gyr. A core passage at least roughly 1 Gyr ago is a travel-time scenario, not a fitted orbit.

**Competing interpretations and classification tension.** Yoon IV and Köppen past imply evolved gas processing, but Paudel establishes satellite interactions and leaves the stripping epoch ambiguous. Nearby NGC4222 has regular H I, so a simple bound-group preprocessing narrative is not demonstrated.

**History confidence.** high for minor accretion; low for gas-loss epoch.

**gFMR/FMR implication — new atlas inference.** Tidal accretion can add stars/gas while slow stripping removes diffuse H I, making global gas and metallicity histories non-monotonic; the streams alone do not demonstrate metal-poor inflow.

**Specific MAUVE-MUSE/VERTICO test.** Use MUSE stellar populations/kinematics at stream interfaces and compare outer-disk gas metallicity to test whether accreted material reached the disk.

**Dedicated-literature status:** No dedicated modern environmental-processing paper was located in the saturated curated search. The history below is constrained by VIVA/phase-space/analytical and multi-survey evidence; confidence is correspondingly limited.

**Adopted mechanism.** none established; possible weak ram pressure. Secondary/contextual process: warp.

**H I evidence (direct morphology first).** The extended, non-deficient H I disk is warped but lacks the bridge or one-sided truncation expected for a strong interaction with nearby NGC 4216.

**CO/H2, ionized gas/UV, stars/dust, radio, X-ray and dynamics.** The stellar disk is comparatively regular. Survey radio/X-ray coverage supplies measurements but no dedicated causal history.

**Best-supported event sequence.** The galaxy is best treated as a minimally processed/weakly perturbed comparison object, with a possible early pressure signature left open.

**Published timing and its meaning.** No dedicated timing constraint.

**Competing interpretations and classification tension.** Yoon class 0 conflicts mildly with Köppen's low-deficiency active assignment; both are low-leverage classifications for this object.

**History confidence.** low.

**gFMR/FMR implication — new atlas inference.** A near-normal gas reservoir makes this a useful control for separating ordinary resolved MZR/SFR structure from removal-driven offsets.

**Specific MAUVE-MUSE/VERTICO test.** Search for faint one-sided ionized gas and compare outer-disk CO/H I truncation radii; do not use phase space alone as an orbit.

## 5.7 NGC4254

| Component | v (km/s) | MAUVE stage | Yoon | Köppen |
|---|---:|---|---:|---|
| NGC4254 | 2453 | pre-peak | I | non-deficient |

### NGC4254: observational record and environmental sequence

**Adopted mechanism.** high-speed tidal encounter/harassment. Secondary/contextual process: weak ongoing ram pressure.

**H I evidence (direct morphology first).** M99 has a strongly asymmetric disk and an approximately 250 kpc star-poor H I stream. The main disk remains H I rich rather than classically truncated.

**CO/H2, ionized gas/UV, stars/dust, radio, X-ray and dynamics.** ALFALFA and numerical work connect the stream to a rapid fly-by; low-frequency radio asymmetry and VESTIGE Halpha knots in stripped gas indicate that ICM interaction may operate simultaneously.

**Best-supported event sequence.** A fast gravitational encounter generated the one-armed response and long tidal debris; subsequent/ongoing weak ram pressure modified diffuse gas and radio plasma without producing severe global deficiency.

**Published timing and its meaning.** Tail models disagree in detail: a combined encounter model gives about 280 Myr since closest passage, whereas the VIRGOHI21 high-speed-tail model gives about 750 Myr. The difference is model-dependent, not a measurement error bar.

**Competing interpretations and classification tension.** Pure RPS cannot readily explain the star-poor 250 kpc feature, while pure tide underpredicts some radio/edge signatures. The weight of evidence favours a compound history; the identity of the perturber remains uncertain.

**History confidence.** high for a past fast encounter; moderate for current RPS.

**gFMR/FMR implication — new atlas inference.** Tidal gas rearrangement and weak stripping can change gas mass, SFE, and local dilution in different directions. A single pre-peak code is therefore insufficient for FMR interpretation.

**Specific MAUVE-MUSE/VERTICO test.** Map abundance and SFE along the one-arm/tail-launch geometry; test whether compressed radio edges correspond to enhanced CO or merely aged cosmic rays.

Grades: 0=no material source; 1=contextual; 2=survey/resolved constraint; 3=direct/tailored strong constraint.

### NGC4293: observational record and environmental sequence

**Dedicated-literature status:** No dedicated modern environmental-processing paper was located in the saturated curated search. The history below is constrained by VIVA/phase-space/analytical and multi-survey evidence; confidence is correspondingly limited.

**Adopted mechanism.** past strong gas stripping. Secondary/contextual process: bar/interaction-driven central gas.

**H I evidence (direct morphology first).** The galaxy is extremely H I deficient with gas confined centrally; absorption and non-circular motions are prominent.

**CO/H2, ionized gas/UV, stars/dust, radio, X-ray and dynamics.** A misaligned/disturbed stellar-gas configuration and strong bar/central activity point to gravitational/internal redistribution in addition to environmental removal.

**Best-supported event sequence.** Outer atomic gas was removed in an earlier passage; surviving gas settled or was driven into the centre by non-axisymmetric dynamics.

**Published timing and its meaning.** No direct stellar clock in the core set; analytical work calls it a past stripper/possible backsplash case.

**Competing interpretations and classification tension.** The gas deficit supports past removal, but the central kinematics cannot be assigned to RPS. Phase-space backsplash is probabilistic.

**History confidence.** moderate-high.

**gFMR/FMR implication — new atlas inference.** Loss of metal-poor outskirts and central aperture weighting can elevate measured metallicity without requiring closed-box enrichment; bar-driven SFE is a separate variable.

**Specific MAUVE-MUSE/VERTICO test.** Measure age/metallicity at the truncation boundary and model bar versus external gas kinematics; avoid AGN/DIG spaxels.

### NGC4294: observational record and environmental sequence

**Dedicated-literature status:** No dedicated modern environmental-processing paper was located in the saturated curated search. The history below is constrained by VIVA/phase-space/analytical and multi-survey evidence; confidence is correspondingly limited.

**Adopted mechanism.** early ram pressure stripping. Secondary/contextual process: tidal interaction with NGC 4299.

**H I evidence (direct morphology first).** A roughly 27 kpc gas-only H I tail and lopsided disk identify recent removal of diffuse gas.

**CO/H2, ionized gas/UV, stars/dust, radio, X-ray and dynamics.** Nearby NGC 4299 and the disturbed stellar/gas geometry leave a tidal contribution plausible; large-scale radio work is consistent with an environmentally perturbed magnetized ISM.

**Best-supported event sequence.** A mild gravitational perturbation may have loosened outer gas, followed by or concurrent with an ICM wind that formed the one-sided tail.

**Published timing and its meaning.** No tailored clock; the tail is a present/recent indicator rather than a quantified time to peak.

**Competing interpretations and classification tension.** Yoon I and Köppen active agree on ongoing/early processing but cannot apportion tide and RPS.

**History confidence.** moderate.

**gFMR/FMR implication — new atlas inference.** Gas loss can reduce H I before SFR responds, moving the galaxy off an H I-based gFMR while leaving the SFR-FMR temporarily less affected.

**Specific MAUVE-MUSE/VERTICO test.** Compare tail-side Halpha/metallicity to the leading half; model the NGC4294/4299 relative orbit before attributing all asymmetry to pressure.

### NGC4298: observational record and environmental sequence

**Dedicated-literature status:** No dedicated modern environmental-processing paper was located in the saturated curated search. The history below is constrained by VIVA/phase-space/analytical and multi-survey evidence; confidence is correspondingly limited.

**Adopted mechanism.** ram pressure plus interaction with NGC4302. Secondary/contextual process: pair tide.

**H I evidence (direct morphology first).** The H I disk is sharply cut on one side and diffuse on the other, with a disturbed distribution in the physical pair.

**CO/H2, ionized gas/UV, stars/dust, radio, X-ray and dynamics.** A compressed radio edge supports an ICM wind; pair geometry and shared disturbances require a tidal contribution.

**Best-supported event sequence.** Mutual gravity perturbed the disks while ram pressure acted on exposed diffuse gas; neither mechanism alone explains every asymmetry.

**Published timing and its meaning.** No tailored event time. The pair's current proximity is observed; a pericentric clock is not.

**Competing interpretations and classification tension.** Yoon II and Köppen active agree on current processing but cannot separate pair and cluster forces.

**History confidence.** high for mixed processing; moderate for chronology.

**gFMR/FMR implication — new atlas inference.** Gas redistribution/compression may raise local SFE even as H I is removed, creating different trajectories in gas-FMR and SFR-FMR space.

**Specific MAUVE-MUSE/VERTICO test.** Jointly model both disks with MUSE stellar/gas velocities, radio leading edges and CO compression; avoid treating pair membership as mere noise.

### NGC4302: observational record and environmental sequence

**Dedicated-literature status:** No dedicated modern environmental-processing paper was located in the saturated curated search. The history below is constrained by VIVA/phase-space/analytical and multi-survey evidence; confidence is correspondingly limited.

**Adopted mechanism.** active ram pressure stripping. Secondary/contextual process: interaction with NGC4298 and disk-halo flows.

**H I evidence (direct morphology first).** The edge-on disk has a southern truncation and northern H I extension/tail.

**CO/H2, ionized gas/UV, stars/dust, radio, X-ray and dynamics.** Diffuse ionized-gas halo and extraplanar material are well measured; radio data support environmental perturbation, while the companion can affect disk structure.

**Best-supported event sequence.** The ICM is displacing outer gas, superposed on an interacting edge-on pair and ordinary fountain/accretion flows.

**Published timing and its meaning.** No robust stripping clock; morphology indicates a current/recent event.

**Competing interpretations and classification tension.** Yoon II and Köppen active converge on ongoing processing. Extraplanar gas is not automatically stripped gas because disk-halo circulation is an alternative.

**History confidence.** moderate-high.

**gFMR/FMR implication — new atlas inference.** Separating fountain gas from stripped gas is essential: recycled metal-rich extraplanar gas and externally removed low-density gas have opposite metallicity implications.

**Specific MAUVE-MUSE/VERTICO test.** Use line ratios, rotation lag and metallicity of extraplanar gas to distinguish fountain, accretion and ICM displacement.

### NGC4321: observational record and environmental sequence

**Dedicated-literature status:** No dedicated modern environmental-processing paper was located in the saturated curated search. The history below is constrained by VIVA/phase-space/analytical and multi-survey evidence; confidence is correspondingly limited.

**Adopted mechanism.** weak tidal perturbation. Secondary/contextual process: possible weak ram pressure.

**H I evidence (direct morphology first).** M100 is H I rich with a southwestern extension/warp and a faint bridge toward NGC 4323; there is no severe truncation.

**CO/H2, ionized gas/UV, stars/dust, radio, X-ray and dynamics.** Rich CO and star-formation data mostly diagnose internal bar/spiral physics. Environmental survey studies supply context but no dedicated stripping history.

**Best-supported event sequence.** A mild companion interaction and/or weak ICM contact perturbed outer atomic gas while leaving the molecular/star-forming disk broadly normal.

**Published timing and its meaning.** No event clock was found.

**Competing interpretations and classification tension.** Yoon 0 classifies no definite stripping, whereas Köppen's analytical selection lists it as low-deficiency active; the observations carry more weight than the analytical tag.

**History confidence.** low-moderate.

**gFMR/FMR implication — new atlas inference.** Internal bar/spiral SFE variations dominate many resolved relations; environmental interpretations require an outer-disk signature that is not yet isolated.

**Specific MAUVE-MUSE/VERTICO test.** Use MAUVE to separate bar/arm/interarm metallicity structure from the southwest H I anomaly and possible companion bridge.

## 5.13 NGC4330

### Identity, catalogue properties and adopted status

| Component | Preferred aliases | log Mstar | log SFR | log MHI | log MH2 | DMS |
|---|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| NGC4330 | VCC 630; UGC 7456; NGC 4330 | 9.61 | -0.86 | 8.67 | 8.20 | -0.49 |

| Component | v (km/s) | MAUVE stage | Yoon | Köppen |
|---|---:|---|---:|---|
| NGC4330 | 1567 | near-peak | II | active |

### NGC4330: observational record and environmental sequence

**Adopted mechanism.** active pre-peak ram pressure stripping. Secondary/contextual process: minor internal asymmetry.

**H I evidence (direct morphology first).** A sharply truncated H I disk and one-sided tail, with undisturbed outer stars, directly identify ongoing outside-in stripping.

**CO/H2, ionized gas/UV, stars/dust, radio, X-ray and dynamics.** GALEX/VESTIGE show a radial quenching gradient; CO is also affected but less strongly. Ionized-gas kinematics trace stripped diffuse material.

**Best-supported event sequence.** Outer gas began disappearing first; the stripping front progressed inward while the galaxy approached peak pressure and continues to quench the disk outside-in.

**Published timing and its meaning.** Tailored modelling places the galaxy about 100 Myr before peak. Resolved stellar-population fits find outer regions quenched roughly 400-600 Myr ago, the Halpha truncation radius about 100 Myr ago, and characteristic local SFR decay times near 50 Myr. These clocks measure different radii/processes.

**Competing interpretations and classification tension.** The model time to pressure peak is not the same as time since quenching at a given radius. Their apparent difference is physically expected in progressive outside-in stripping.

**History confidence.** high.

**gFMR/FMR implication — new atlas inference.** NGC4330 provides a time-ordered test: gas fraction falls first, local SFR follows quickly at each radius, and metallicity can rise through halted dilution and radial-selection effects.

**Specific MAUVE-MUSE/VERTICO test.** Measure abundance/SFE across the dated quenching front and along the tail, with explicit radius-dependent clocks rather than one galaxy-wide stage.

| NGC4351 | 2388 | pre-peak | I | past |

### NGC4351: observational record and environmental sequence

**Dedicated-literature status:** No dedicated modern environmental-processing paper was located in the saturated curated search. The history below is constrained by VIVA/phase-space/analytical and multi-survey evidence; confidence is correspondingly limited.

**Adopted mechanism.** active/early ram pressure stripping. Secondary/contextual process: possible gravitational perturbation.

**H I evidence (direct morphology first).** A sharp H I cutoff and short one-sided tail, together with a compressed edge, indicate current pressure.

**CO/H2, ionized gas/UV, stars/dust, radio, X-ray and dynamics.** Radio compression supports an ICM wind; shell-like/offset stellar structure means gravity may also have disturbed the disk.

**Best-supported event sequence.** A galaxy on a high-velocity radial approach is losing its outer atomic gas now; a prior gravitational perturbation may explain the stellar asymmetry.

**Published timing and its meaning.** No tailored numerical time; the morphology constrains an active stage only.

**Competing interpretations and classification tension.** Yoon I indicates early stripping, while Köppen assigns a past-strip solution. The disagreement shows how analytical deficiency fits can conflict with direct present morphology.

**History confidence.** moderate-high for active RPS; low for prior tide.

**gFMR/FMR implication — new atlas inference.** A leading-edge compression episode can transiently raise local SFR/SFE while global H I falls, so local and global FMR residuals need not share a sign.

**Specific MAUVE-MUSE/VERTICO test.** Test shocked/DIG gas at the compressed edge and derive stellar-versus-gas kinematic asymmetry to isolate a gravitational component.

### NGC4380: observational record and environmental sequence

**Dedicated-literature status:** No dedicated modern environmental-processing paper was located in the saturated curated search. The history below is constrained by VIVA/phase-space/analytical and multi-survey evidence; confidence is correspondingly limited.

**Adopted mechanism.** old/slow gas depletion. Secondary/contextual process: starvation or weak past stripping.

**H I evidence (direct morphology first).** H I is mildly asymmetric, low surface brightness and deficient but lacks an active one-sided wind morphology.

**CO/H2, ionized gas/UV, stars/dust, radio, X-ray and dynamics.** The disk is anemic; CO and radio surveys provide measurements without a dedicated event reconstruction.

**Best-supported event sequence.** A slow process or relaxed remnant of an older stripping event reduced diffuse gas and star formation without leaving a strong current tail.

**Published timing and its meaning.** No direct clock.

**Competing interpretations and classification tension.** Yoon IV supports slow processes; Köppen labels past stripping. Current observations do not discriminate starvation, evaporation, or relaxed RPS.

**History confidence.** moderate for processed status; low for mechanism.

**gFMR/FMR implication — new atlas inference.** A slow fall in gas supply can move the system closer to equilibrium than impulsive stripping; radial aperture loss can still mimic metallicity enhancement.

**Specific MAUVE-MUSE/VERTICO test.** Use stellar ages and outer-disk abundance gradients to distinguish prolonged starvation from an abrupt old truncation.

## 5.16 NGC4383

### Identity, catalogue properties and adopted status

| NGC4383 | VCC 801; UGC 7507; NGC 4383 | 9.42 | -0.01 | 9.49 | 8.24 | 0.48 |

| Component | v (km/s) | MAUVE stage | Yoon | Köppen |
|---|---:|---|---:|---|
| NGC4383 | 1663 | pre-peak | 0 | non-deficient |

### NGC4383: observational record and environmental sequence

**Adopted mechanism.** recent gas acquisition and starburst feedback. Secondary/contextual process: tidal perturbation.

**H I evidence (direct morphology first).** The H I disk is exceptionally massive and extended, with irregular outer clouds rather than a stripped/truncated morphology.

**CO/H2, ionized gas/UV, stars/dust, radio, X-ray and dynamics.** MAUVE-MUSE resolves a roughly 6 kpc bipolar ionized outflow. The galaxy is a metallicity outlier below the ensemble rMZR; this is consistent with recent gas-supply disturbance but does not identify the donor or orbit.

**Best-supported event sequence.** External gas acquisition or a tidal perturbation plausibly fuelled a central starburst, which launched the observed wind; direct Virgo ICM stripping is not the dominant observed process.

**Published timing and its meaning.** Outflow dynamical times are Myr-scale and source-dependent; no secure cluster-pericentre time exists. Do not equate the wind age with the interaction age.

**Competing interpretations and classification tension.** Yoon 0 and Köppen non-deficient agree against strong stripping. The accretion trigger is an inference from H I richness, morphology and metallicity, not a directly identified encounter.

**History confidence.** high for feedback outflow; moderate for recent gas supply; low for orbital history.

**gFMR/FMR implication — new atlas inference.** This is the clearest case where gas accretion/dilution and non-disk ionized emission can drive a gFMR/FMR residual independently of cluster stage; Halpha may not be a clean in-situ SFR proxy in the wind.

**Specific MAUVE-MUSE/VERTICO test.** Use CO/H I metallicity mixing maps, separate disk HII regions from outflow spaxels, and search deep imaging/kinematics for an accretion donor.

## 5.17 NGC4388

### Identity, catalogue properties and adopted status

| Component | Preferred aliases | log Mstar | log SFR | log MHI | log MH2 | DMS |
|---|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| NGC4388 | VCC 836; UGC 7520; NGC 4388 | 10.05 | 0.38 | 8.59 | 8.75 | 0.46 |

| Component | v (km/s) | MAUVE stage | Yoon | Köppen |
|---|---:|---|---:|---|
| NGC4388 | 2538 | near-peak | II | active |

### NGC4388: observational record and environmental sequence

**Adopted mechanism.** strong recent ram pressure stripping. Secondary/contextual process: AGN illumination/outflow.

**H I evidence (direct morphology first).** The severely truncated disk feeds a roughly 110 x 25 kpc, star-poor H I plume containing about 3.4e8 Msun; this is among the clearest direct gas-removal cases.

**CO/H2, ionized gas/UV, stars/dust, radio, X-ray and dynamics.** The 35 kpc ionized VEELR is mostly stripped gas photoionized by the Seyfert nucleus, with shocks in outer/high-velocity regions. X-ray absorption places the H I in front of M86 and favours the main Virgo/M87 atmosphere; CO about 70 kpc down-tail shows inefficient in-situ molecule formation.

**Best-supported event sequence.** A high-pressure encounter removed most outer gas; stripped multiphase material persists far downstream while the active nucleus illuminates, shocks or drives physically distinct ionized components.

**Published timing and its meaning.** The 2009 model is about 150 Myr after peak; updated tailored work gives about 240 Myr after peak, while outer-disk SFHs give 190+/-30 Myr since quenching. These are different clocks, and the tailored peak has no formal statistical error.

**Competing interpretations and classification tension.** MAUVE/Yoon near-peak grouping is morphological, while tailored work is post-peak. Later X-ray geometry weakens the earlier tentative M86-halo origin, though local structure can contribute. No single model fits every disk/tail observable; AGN emission must not be counted as stripped-gas star formation.

**History confidence.** very high for stripping; high-moderate for exact ICM/orbit.

**gFMR/FMR implication — new atlas inference.** Gas loss is extreme, but line ratios are AGN contaminated; SFR-FMR work needs strict HII selection. Tail depletion times of about 1.6e10 to >1.4e12 yr show that gas presence does not imply efficient star formation.

**Specific MAUVE-MUSE/VERTICO test.** Separate AGN cone, shocks, extraplanar tail and disk HII emission spectroscopically; use metallicity/velocity continuity to identify stripped parcels.

### NGC4394: observational record and environmental sequence

**Dedicated-literature status:** No dedicated modern environmental-processing paper was located in the saturated curated search. The history below is constrained by VIVA/phase-space/analytical and multi-survey evidence; confidence is correspondingly limited.

**Adopted mechanism.** slow/old gas depletion. Secondary/contextual process: possible weak M85 tide.

**H I evidence (direct morphology first).** The H I disk is deficient and mildly truncated but strikingly symmetric.

**CO/H2, ionized gas/UV, stars/dust, radio, X-ray and dynamics.** The galaxy is anemic. Although projected near M85, VIVA reports no decisive tidal signature linking the two.

**Best-supported event sequence.** Gas supply/removal acted gently or long enough ago for the disk to relax; no active stripping evidence remains.

**Published timing and its meaning.** No published event clock and no Köppen entry.

**Competing interpretations and classification tension.** Yoon IV supplies only a morphology class. Association with M85 is contextual, not causal evidence.

**History confidence.** low-moderate.

**gFMR/FMR implication — new atlas inference.** This is a useful test of slow gas exhaustion: lower gas and SFR may remain coupled, unlike impulsive stripping, but aperture/radial effects must be controlled.

**Specific MAUVE-MUSE/VERTICO test.** Derive spatially resolved SFHs and gas-star misalignment; search for subtle M85-facing age or metallicity asymmetry.

### NGC4396: observational record and environmental sequence

**Dedicated-literature status:** No dedicated modern environmental-processing paper was located in the saturated curated search. The history below is constrained by VIVA/phase-space/analytical and multi-survey evidence; confidence is correspondingly limited.

**Adopted mechanism.** early ram pressure stripping. Secondary/contextual process: possible high-speed tide.

**H I evidence (direct morphology first).** A long northwestern H I tail and gas-free outer stellar disk favour removal of diffuse gas.

**CO/H2, ionized gas/UV, stars/dust, radio, X-ray and dynamics.** The radio continuum does not show every expected wind diagnostic, so the tail geometry is not uniquely hydrodynamic; MAUVE ionized-gas data provide a current disk-state measurement.

**Best-supported event sequence.** Recent outer-disk gas loss is likely; a fast encounter may have contributed, but no perturber is securely tied to the event.

**Published timing and its meaning.** No tailored timing constraint.

**Competing interpretations and classification tension.** Yoon I and Köppen active support ongoing processing, while the incomplete radio signature lowers mechanism confidence.

**History confidence.** moderate.

**gFMR/FMR implication — new atlas inference.** Outer gas removal should first change gas fraction and resolved radial sampling; an integrated metallicity increase could reflect loss of low-metallicity outskirts rather than rapid enrichment.

**Specific MAUVE-MUSE/VERTICO test.** Measure tail-side gas excitation and metallicity, and compare gas/stellar residual fields for a tidal impulse.

### NGC4405: observational record and environmental sequence

**Adopted mechanism.** past ram pressure stripping. Secondary/contextual process: none clearly established.

**H I evidence (direct morphology first).** The H I disk is severely and symmetrically truncated while the stellar disk is regular, with little evidence for current pressure.

**CO/H2, ionized gas/UV, stars/dust, radio, X-ray and dynamics.** Outer-disk stellar populations are quenched; broad CO/survey data do not identify a competing gravitational event.

**Best-supported event sequence.** An earlier ICM passage removed outer gas; the remaining inner disk has since relaxed.

**Published timing and its meaning.** Outer-disk quenching is about 450+/-150 Myr.

**Competing interpretations and classification tension.** Yoon III and Köppen past agree. The clock dates cessation of local star formation, not necessarily exact pressure maximum.

**History confidence.** high.

**gFMR/FMR implication — new atlas inference.** A post-stripping system should show low gas fraction and radially censored star formation; metallicity offsets can arise from outside-in removal and halted dilution.

**Specific MAUVE-MUSE/VERTICO test.** Measure metallicity/ages immediately inside and outside the old truncation radius and search for fallback gas.

### NGC4402: observational record and environmental sequence

**Adopted mechanism.** active ram pressure stripping. Secondary/contextual process: dense-cloud decoupling.

**H I evidence (direct morphology first).** A compressed southeastern edge, sharp H I truncation and northwestern extraplanar tail show an ICM wind acting now.

**CO/H2, ionized gas/UV, stars/dust, radio, X-ray and dynamics.** Dust filaments reveal ablation and dense-cloud decoupling; ALMA detects compressed and extraplanar molecular gas. Radio polarization/deficit patterns independently locate the windward edge.

**Best-supported event sequence.** Diffuse outer gas was stripped first; denser clouds partially decoupled and are now ablated, while even molecular gas is compressed/displaced.

**Published timing and its meaning.** Outer-disk stellar work gives less than about 200 Myr since local quenching; no single tailored time to peak is as secure as for NGC4330/4501/4522.

**Competing interpretations and classification tension.** The current wind is certain, but individual extraplanar dense clouds may be transiently bound or fall back; molecular removal is not uniform.

**History confidence.** high.

**gFMR/FMR implication — new atlas inference.** This is a direct demonstration that environment can alter both gas mass and local SFE. Dense surviving clouds and stripped diffuse gas should occupy different resolved gFMR pathways.

**Specific MAUVE-MUSE/VERTICO test.** Pair cloud-scale CO, extinction and metallicity with leading/trailing MUSE maps; identify bound versus escaping components kinematically.

## 5.22 NGC4419

| NGC4419 | -228 | post-peak | III | past |

### NGC4419: observational record and environmental sequence

**Adopted mechanism.** past strong ram pressure stripping. Secondary/contextual process: central bar/nuclear processing.

**H I evidence (direct morphology first).** The H I disk is extremely small relative to the stars; extraplanar/absorption features suggest residual displacement or fallback.

**CO/H2, ionized gas/UV, stars/dust, radio, X-ray and dynamics.** The stellar disk is comparatively undisturbed. CO is centrally concentrated and historical observations show asymmetry; MAUVE ionized-gas data sample a strongly processed disk.

**Best-supported event sequence.** A strong event removed outer gas; surviving/fallback material and central dynamics now dominate the inner disk.

**Published timing and its meaning.** Outer-disk quenching is about 500+/-150 Myr (individual indicators vary). No current pressure maximum is inferred.

**Competing interpretations and classification tension.** The regular stellar disk favours hydrodynamic removal, while central non-axisymmetry may be internal. Negative line-of-sight velocity makes phase-space placement projection sensitive.

**History confidence.** high.

**gFMR/FMR implication — new atlas inference.** Strong radial truncation changes which metallicities enter integrated measurements; central gas retention can preserve SFR at very low H I mass.

**Specific MAUVE-MUSE/VERTICO test.** Search for kinematically distinct fallback gas and compare central molecular SFE to the quenched outer-disk age gradient.

Grades: 0=no material source; 1=contextual; 2=survey/resolved constraint; 3=direct/tailored strong constraint.

## 5.23 NGC4424

| Component | Preferred aliases | log Mstar | log SFR | log MHI | log MH2 | DMS |

| NGC4424 | VCC 979; UGC 7561; NGC 4424 | 9.90 | -0.51 | 8.31 | 8.18 | -0.34 |

| Component | v (km/s) | MAUVE stage | Yoon | Köppen |
|---|---:|---|---:|---|
| NGC4424 | 447 | near-peak | II | active |

### NGC4424: observational record and environmental sequence

**Adopted mechanism.** intermediate-ratio merger remnant plus ongoing ram pressure. Secondary/contextual process: central starburst outflow.

**H I evidence (direct morphology first).** A roughly 110 kpc one-sided H I tail and strongly disturbed central gas coexist with a shell/banana-shaped stellar body.

**CO/H2, ionized gas/UV, stars/dust, radio, X-ray and dynamics.** Low V/sigma, possible gas counter-rotation and non-circular CO establish a recent roughly 1:4-1:10 merger. VESTIGE finds outside-in Halpha truncation and an RPS tail, while the opposite 10 kpc NW ionized feature is a shock-excited starburst outflow supported by radio polarization.

**Best-supported event sequence.** A gravitational encounter transformed the stellar disk and concentrated gas; ongoing near-face-on ram pressure removed low-density material, while the central burst powered a distinct outflow.

**Published timing and its meaning.** West/east outer-disk quenching times are 281+/-27 and 251+/-14 Myr; a simple central SFH event is 179+/-68 Myr. The merger is inferred within roughly 500 Myr, not dynamically dated.

**Competing interpretations and classification tension.** Yoon II and Köppen active classify the gas morphology but omit the prior merger. The southern stripping tail and NW outflow are physically different; treating all extraplanar gas as one mechanism is wrong.

**History confidence.** high for compound history; moderate for detailed ordering.

**gFMR/FMR implication — new atlas inference.** Merger-driven inflow/mixing and selective outer removal can be simultaneous; shock/outflow spaxels and non-HII excitation must be excluded before interpreting metallicity residuals.

**Specific MAUVE-MUSE/VERTICO test.** Reconstruct stellar populations and metallicity along shells, central inflow and both tails; use gas-star misalignment to time gravity separately from stripping.

Grades: 0=no material source; 1=contextual; 2=survey/resolved constraint; 3=direct/tailored strong constraint.

### NGC4450: observational record and environmental sequence

**Dedicated-literature status:** No dedicated modern environmental-processing paper was located in the saturated curated search. The history below is constrained by VIVA/phase-space/analytical and multi-survey evidence; confidence is correspondingly limited.

**Adopted mechanism.** old/slow environmental depletion. Secondary/contextual process: bar/AGN/internal secular evolution.

**H I evidence (direct morphology first).** H I is moderately truncated and low surface density but symmetric, with no current tail or compressed edge.

**CO/H2, ionized gas/UV, stars/dust, radio, X-ray and dynamics.** Anemic star formation coexists with a bar and low-luminosity AGN; these internal processes complicate ionized-gas interpretation.

**Best-supported event sequence.** Diffuse gas was slowly removed/starved or stripped long ago; the present gas disk is relaxed.

**Published timing and its meaning.** No direct quenching or pressure clock.

**Competing interpretations and classification tension.** Yoon IV and Köppen past agree on an evolved state but not on mechanism. AGN emission is not evidence of environment.

**History confidence.** moderate-low.

**gFMR/FMR implication — new atlas inference.** Slow depletion and AGN/DIG contamination make Halpha-based SFR especially vulnerable; gas-based relations may better record the environmental deficit.

**Specific MAUVE-MUSE/VERTICO test.** Use HII-only masks, stellar ages and outer CO/H I limits to distinguish starvation from old impulsive stripping.

### NGC4457: observational record and environmental sequence

**Dedicated-literature status:** No dedicated modern environmental-processing paper was located in the saturated curated search. The history below is constrained by VIVA/phase-space/analytical and multi-survey evidence; confidence is correspondingly limited.

**Adopted mechanism.** past/relaxed gas stripping. Secondary/contextual process: warp or minor accretion.

**H I evidence (direct morphology first).** The H I disk is small and asymmetric/warped but lacks a strong current wind signature.

**CO/H2, ionized gas/UV, stars/dust, radio, X-ray and dynamics.** Radio and MAUVE ionized-gas surveys reveal a processed disk but no dedicated orbital model; internal structure remains plausible.

**Best-supported event sequence.** An earlier mild-to-moderate removal episode and/or minor accretion disturbed gas; the present event status is relaxed.

**Published timing and its meaning.** No published clock; Köppen does not include the galaxy.

**Competing interpretations and classification tension.** Yoon III implies severe/past morphology, but VIVA describes a less decisive active signature. Mechanism confidence is limited.

**History confidence.** moderate-low.

**gFMR/FMR implication — new atlas inference.** Gas warps/accretion can change metallicity and kinematics independently of stripping, so a post label alone cannot set the expected FMR residual.

**Specific MAUVE-MUSE/VERTICO test.** Test gas-star misalignment and azimuthal metallicity asymmetry; determine whether the warp carries distinct abundance.

## 5.26 NGC4501

### NGC4501: observational record and environmental sequence

**Adopted mechanism.** pre-peak ram pressure stripping. Secondary/contextual process: internal spiral/bar response.

**H I evidence (direct morphology first).** A compressed southwest/southeast H I edge and extended opposite side provide a textbook early-stage wind geometry.

**CO/H2, ionized gas/UV, stars/dust, radio, X-ray and dynamics.** Polarized radio ridge and MAUVE ionized-gas asymmetry support current compression; CO studies show multiphase response rather than simple atomic-only loss.

**Best-supported event sequence.** The galaxy is approaching maximum pressure; the leading side is compressed while diffuse gas is displaced to the trailing side, before severe symmetric truncation.

**Published timing and its meaning.** Tailored models place it roughly 200-300 Myr before peak (Vollmer 2009 adopts -250 Myr). Model variants span about 190-320 Myr and depend on pressure profile/orientation.

**Competing interpretations and classification tension.** Projected phase space cannot by itself fix the clock; the tailored multi-tracer fit is stronger but still model-dependent. Compression need not imply globally enhanced star formation.

**History confidence.** high.

**gFMR/FMR implication — new atlas inference.** This is the clean pre-peak comparator: pressure can redistribute gas and DIG before major gas depletion, potentially altering local SFE/SFR while total gas remains substantial.

**Specific MAUVE-MUSE/VERTICO test.** Test leading-versus-trailing metallicity, DIG fraction and CO excitation against the model wind vector; compare with NGC4522 near peak.

## 5.27 NGC4522

### Identity, catalogue properties and adopted status

| NGC4522 | VCC 1516; UGC 7711; NGC 4522 | 9.64 | -0.80 | 8.56 | 8.17 | -0.45 |

| Component | v (km/s) | MAUVE stage | Yoon | Köppen |
|---|---:|---|---:|---|
| NGC4522 | 2332 | near-peak | II | active |

### NGC4522: observational record and environmental sequence

**Adopted mechanism.** near/post-peak ram pressure stripping. Secondary/contextual process: dense-cloud decoupling and ICM-ISM mixing.

**H I evidence (direct morphology first).** The H I disk is truncated to roughly 0.35 optical radii with abundant extraplanar gas while the stellar disk is undisturbed.

**CO/H2, ionized gas/UV, stars/dust, radio, X-ray and dynamics.** HST dust, CO, radio and MUSE all show direct multiphase stripping. New high-resolution radio spectra indicate shock re-acceleration and mixing; extraplanar thermal emission is not solely star formation.

**Best-supported event sequence.** A strong ICM encounter compressed the leading edge, removed diffuse and some molecular gas, and left dense clouds temporarily decoupled in the extraplanar flow.

**Published timing and its meaning.** Tailored modelling gives about 50 Myr after pressure maximum; outer-disk stellar quenching is about 100+/-50 Myr. The pressure excess requires local ICM substructure or relative ICM motion.

**Competing interpretations and classification tension.** The adopted near-peak label covers an object that best-fit models place just after peak. Static smooth-ICM models underpredict the required pressure, so orbit-only interpretations are inadequate.

**History confidence.** very high.

**gFMR/FMR implication — new atlas inference.** Because shocks/mixing add ionized emission and CO-bearing clouds survive, both Halpha SFR and gas mass require phase-aware measurement. The galaxy is a benchmark for non-equilibrium gFMR tracks.

**Specific MAUVE-MUSE/VERTICO test.** Separate HII, shock/mixing and DIG components; measure cloud-by-cloud metallicity, CO, and escape/fallback kinematics across the wind interface.

### NGC4535: observational record and environmental sequence

**Dedicated-literature status:** No dedicated modern environmental-processing paper was located in the saturated curated search. The history below is constrained by VIVA/phase-space/analytical and multi-survey evidence; confidence is correspondingly limited.

**Adopted mechanism.** weak ongoing ram pressure. Secondary/contextual process: internal spiral structure.

**H I evidence (direct morphology first).** The H I disk is extended and close to normal but has a cutoff on the M87-facing side and an extension on the opposite side.

**CO/H2, ionized gas/UV, stars/dust, radio, X-ray and dynamics.** Radio asymmetry offers weak support for pressure; abundant PHANGS/VERTICO measurements otherwise emphasize internal disk dynamics.

**Best-supported event sequence.** A low-level ICM wind is beginning to reshape outer atomic gas without wholesale depletion.

**Published timing and its meaning.** No tailored clock.

**Competing interpretations and classification tension.** Yoon I and Köppen active are consistent, but both evidence and effect size are modest.

**History confidence.** moderate-low.

**gFMR/FMR implication — new atlas inference.** This system tests the earliest regime where gas geometry changes before large SFR or metallicity offsets emerge.

**Specific MAUVE-MUSE/VERTICO test.** Target leading/trailing HII-region abundances and DIG, and compare CO/H I edge radii at matched sensitivity.

### NGC4548: observational record and environmental sequence

**Dedicated-literature status:** No dedicated modern environmental-processing paper was located in the saturated curated search. The history below is constrained by VIVA/phase-space/analytical and multi-survey evidence; confidence is correspondingly limited.

**Adopted mechanism.** slow or old stripping/starvation. Secondary/contextual process: bar dynamics.

**H I evidence (direct morphology first).** The H I disk is mildly truncated and low surface density; it is an anemic galaxy without a convincing present wind tail.

**CO/H2, ionized gas/UV, stars/dust, radio, X-ray and dynamics.** A strong bar dominates gas kinematics. LOFAR supplies environmental radio context but does not yield a unique stripping history.

**Best-supported event sequence.** Gas supply was reduced gradually or in an older event; the surviving disk evolved secularly under the bar.

**Published timing and its meaning.** No robust time. Earlier kinematic work explicitly questioned whether stripping is still active.

**Competing interpretations and classification tension.** Yoon IV and Köppen past support an evolved state, while direct evidence cannot choose starvation versus relaxed RPS.

**History confidence.** moderate-low.

**gFMR/FMR implication — new atlas inference.** Bar-driven SFE and slow gas depletion are degenerate in SFR-based FMR projections; spatial gas mass and stellar ages are needed.

**Specific MAUVE-MUSE/VERTICO test.** Model bar streaming before searching for weak wind residuals; compare outer-disk ages to gas truncation.

This is one MAUVE target system but two physical galaxies. Both are Yoon class 0/pre-peak in the adopted bookkeeping. Every evidence and property record remains component-specific; only the shared collision geometry is synthesized jointly.

### NGC4567: observational record and environmental sequence

**Adopted mechanism.** direct galaxy-galaxy collision. Secondary/contextual process: no strong ICM stripping established.

**H I evidence (direct morphology first).** H I connects the pair; assigning integrated H I flux to either component is uncertain in the overlap.

**CO/H2, ionized gas/UV, stars/dust, radio, X-ray and dynamics.** CO and H I show enhanced molecular fraction in the overlap. ALMA resolves a continuous roughly 50 km/s connection and a 1.8 x 0.35 kpc molecular collision-front filament, but later line ratios indicate low excitation rather than a mature dense starburst ridge.

**Best-supported event sequence.** NGC4567 and NGC4568 are undergoing first close contact; localized gas compression and phase conversion precede any merger remnant or global starburst.

**Published timing and its meaning.** The encounter is early/pre-coalescence, but no defensible absolute pericentre time or Virgo orbital clock is published; cloud free-fall times are not encounter ages.

**Competing interpretations and classification tension.** The system is Yoon 0 and absent from Köppen. Integrated SFR/SFE remains normal within scatter, so a collision front is not equivalent to global enhancement; cluster RPS is not securely established.

**History confidence.** high for pair interaction; no secure pair or cluster clock.

**gFMR/FMR implication — new atlas inference.** Localized H I-to-H2 conversion and mixing can affect resolved gFMR projections, but there is no direct abundance-dilution measurement and no demonstrated galaxy-wide SFE enhancement.

**Specific MAUVE-MUSE/VERTICO test.** Decompose MUSE velocities and metallicities by component and overlap; keep NGC4567 and NGC4568 gas budgets separate wherever the data allow.

### NGC4568: observational record and environmental sequence

**Adopted mechanism.** direct galaxy-galaxy collision. Secondary/contextual process: tidal compression/shear; no strong ICM stripping established.

**H I evidence (direct morphology first).** H I bridges the pair, but component fluxes are blended; NGC4568 has its own catalogued gas and stellar properties.

**CO/H2, ionized gas/UV, stars/dust, radio, X-ray and dynamics.** The molecular collision front is gravitationally bound but low excitation (maximum CO(3-2)/CO(1-0) about 0.17+/-0.04). Radio polarization is consistent with tidal compression/shear rather than strong ongoing RPS.

**Best-supported event sequence.** NGC4568 participates in the same first-contact event, with localized phase conversion/compression rather than evidence for global stripping or a mature overlap starburst.

**Published timing and its meaning.** The encounter is early/pre-coalescence; no secure absolute encounter time or Virgo pericentre time is published.

**Competing interpretations and classification tension.** Yoon 0 and no Köppen entry support leaving the ICM history unclassified. Resolved SFE is at most slightly enhanced in the overlap and the pair-wide value is normal within uncertainty.

**History confidence.** high for pair interaction; no secure pair or cluster clock.

**gFMR/FMR implication — new atlas inference.** Local compression/mixing can move spaxels across resolved FMR projections without implying an ICM sequence; direct oxygen-abundance evidence is still needed.

**Specific MAUVE-MUSE/VERTICO test.** Fit two stellar/gas velocity systems and exclude overlap spaxels when establishing each component's baseline resolved MZR and SFE.

| NGC4569 | -220 | post-peak | III | past |

### NGC4569: observational record and environmental sequence

**Adopted mechanism.** past strong ram pressure stripping. Secondary/contextual process: nuclear starburst/outflow.

**H I evidence (direct morphology first).** The H I disk is only about one third of the stellar extent and is highly deficient; extraplanar material and a newly traced 230 kpc Halpha tail record removed gas.

**CO/H2, ionized gas/UV, stars/dust, radio, X-ray and dynamics.** A magnetized/nuclear outflow is distinct from the stripping tail. Spectroscopy along the 230 kpc tail shows a smooth velocity gradient toward the Virgo systemic velocity, directly tracing deceleration of stripped ISM.

**Best-supported event sequence.** A major pressure event removed the outer disk about 0.3 Gyr ago; a central wind developed from surviving gas while stripped clouds mixed with and decelerated in the ICM.

**Published timing and its meaning.** Tailored gas models and outer stellar populations both give about 300 Myr since peak/quenching (stellar uncertainty about 50 Myr). The far Halpha tail implies cloud survival at least about 0.5 Gyr under model assumptions.

**Competing interpretations and classification tension.** The nuclear outflow contains far less mass than the stripped component and must not be used to explain the global deficiency. The 230 kpc tail strengthens RPS but cloud survival/mixing physics remain uncertain.

**History confidence.** very high.

**gFMR/FMR implication — new atlas inference.** This galaxy combines genuine gas removal, long-lived ionized tail emission and a central wind; Halpha outside the disk is not in-situ SFR, while gas fraction strongly departs from equilibrium expectations.

**Specific MAUVE-MUSE/VERTICO test.** Use tail metallicity/line ratios and MUSE disk SFHs to connect the 300 Myr event to present central SFE; exclude wind/tail emission from disk FMR fits.

Grades: 0=no material source; 1=contextual; 2=survey/resolved constraint; 3=direct/tailored strong constraint.

### NGC4579: observational record and environmental sequence

**Dedicated-literature status:** No dedicated modern environmental-processing paper was located in the saturated curated search. The history below is constrained by VIVA/phase-space/analytical and multi-survey evidence; confidence is correspondingly limited.

**Adopted mechanism.** old/slow gas depletion. Secondary/contextual process: bar and AGN.

**H I evidence (direct morphology first).** The H I disk is moderately and symmetrically truncated, without a clear current tail.

**CO/H2, ionized gas/UV, stars/dust, radio, X-ray and dynamics.** A bar and LINER/Seyfert nucleus dominate central molecular and ionized-gas behavior.

**Best-supported event sequence.** Environmental gas loss/starvation acted in the past or slowly; the present disk is relaxed and central secular/AGN processes now dominate.

**Published timing and its meaning.** No environmental clock.

**Competing interpretations and classification tension.** Yoon IV and Köppen past agree only at the broad level. AGN/torque studies are not environmental evidence.

**History confidence.** moderate-low.

**gFMR/FMR implication — new atlas inference.** AGN/DIG and bar inflow contaminate SFR/metallicity diagnostics; gas-based deficits should be interpreted separately from central emission.

**Specific MAUVE-MUSE/VERTICO test.** Apply conservative HII masks and compare outer stellar ages to H I/CO truncation, after subtracting bar streaming.

### NGC4580: observational record and environmental sequence

**Adopted mechanism.** past ram pressure stripping/backsplash candidate. Secondary/contextual process: none clearly established.

**H I evidence (direct morphology first).** A severely truncated H I disk sits inside a regular stellar disk with no present tail.

**CO/H2, ionized gas/UV, stars/dust, radio, X-ray and dynamics.** Outer-disk stellar populations are quenched; the retained central disk continues limited star formation.

**Best-supported event sequence.** A past ICM event stripped the outer disk, after which the galaxy moved to its current projected location and the gas morphology relaxed.

**Published timing and its meaning.** Outer-disk quenching is about 475+/-100 Myr. Backsplash status is a phase-space/model inference, not directly observed.

**Competing interpretations and classification tension.** Yoon III and Köppen past converge. The current large projected radius does not uniquely prove a core passage.

**History confidence.** high for past stripping; moderate for backsplash.

**gFMR/FMR implication — new atlas inference.** The galaxy is a strong post-event test of how metallicity and SFE evolve for roughly half a Gyr after abrupt outside-in gas loss.

**Specific MAUVE-MUSE/VERTICO test.** Derive a resolved age-metallicity profile across the fossil truncation and search for reaccreted/fallback gas.

### NGC4606: observational record and environmental sequence

**Dedicated-literature status:** No dedicated modern environmental-processing paper was located in the saturated curated search. The history below is constrained by VIVA/phase-space/analytical and multi-survey evidence; confidence is correspondingly limited.

**Adopted mechanism.** past severe gas stripping. Secondary/contextual process: gravitational disturbance.

**H I evidence (direct morphology first).** H I occupies only a small fraction of the stellar disk and is strongly deficient.

**CO/H2, ionized gas/UV, stars/dust, radio, X-ray and dynamics.** The stellar morphology is disturbed, so tides/harassment likely contributed; no dedicated tailored model fixes the balance.

**Best-supported event sequence.** A gravitational perturbation altered the stellar disk and environmental pressure removed most diffuse gas, in uncertain order.

**Published timing and its meaning.** No direct clock; Köppen treats it as a past stripper.

**Competing interpretations and classification tension.** Yoon III and Köppen past support an evolved gas state, but a pure-RPS narrative is inconsistent with the disturbed stars.

**History confidence.** moderate.

**gFMR/FMR implication — new atlas inference.** Tidal inflow and gas stripping can offset one another in SFR and metallicity, making an integrated post-peak residual non-unique.

**Specific MAUVE-MUSE/VERTICO test.** Use stellar/gas kinematic misalignment and SFHs to order the tidal and stripping events; constrain remaining central CO SFE.

### NGC4607: observational record and environmental sequence

**Dedicated-literature status:** No dedicated modern environmental-processing paper was located in the saturated curated search. The history below is constrained by VIVA/phase-space/analytical and multi-survey evidence; confidence is correspondingly limited.

**Adopted mechanism.** past or ongoing ram pressure stripping. Secondary/contextual process: projected association with NGC4606.

**H I evidence (direct morphology first).** The edge-on H I disk is truncated and deficient.

**CO/H2, ionized gas/UV, stars/dust, radio, X-ray and dynamics.** The approximately 600 km/s velocity difference and lack of a clear tidal bridge weaken the idea that NGC4606 is a bound perturber. LOFAR identifies NGC4607 as a new radio-RPS candidate.

**Best-supported event sequence.** ICM interaction likely truncated the disk; apparent pairing with NGC4606 may be mostly projection rather than the cause.

**Published timing and its meaning.** No tailored time.

**Competing interpretations and classification tension.** Yoon III/Köppen past imply a relaxed event, whereas current radio asymmetry may indicate residual/renewed pressure. This is a real status tension, not an error.

**History confidence.** moderate.

**gFMR/FMR implication — new atlas inference.** Radio evidence can persist or reveal current pressure after H I truncation; gas mass and SFR clocks may therefore be asynchronous.

**Specific MAUVE-MUSE/VERTICO test.** Use MUSE DIG/shock asymmetry and radio spectral aging to decide whether pressure is still active; test the pair hypothesis with stellar velocities.

## 5.36 NGC4654
