| NGC4654 | VCC 1987; UGC 7902; IC 3708; NGC 4654 | 10.24 | 0.29 | 9.50 | 9.19 | 0.24 |

| Component | v (km/s) | MAUVE stage | Yoon | Köppen |
|---|---:|---|---:|---|
| NGC4654 | 1035 | near-peak | II | non-deficient |

### NGC4654: observational record and environmental sequence

**Adopted mechanism.** tidal interaction plus weak ram pressure. Secondary/contextual process: stellar feedback.

**H I evidence (direct morphology first).** A long southeastern H I tail and northwestern compressed edge cannot be reproduced simultaneously by a single simple perturbation.

**CO/H2, ionized gas/UV, stars/dust, radio, X-ray and dynamics.** Models and polarized radio data favour a close tidal encounter with NGC4639 about 500 Myr ago plus weak ongoing pressure; resolved CO/SF work adds feedback and gas-compression constraints.

**Best-supported event sequence.** Tides pulled gas outward and generated asymmetric stellar/gas structure; weak ram pressure then acted efficiently on the loosened gas and continues to shape the outer arm.

**Published timing and its meaning.** The preferred model places the NGC4639 encounter about 500 Myr ago; ongoing pressure is weak. This is a tidal clock, not time from Virgo pressure peak.

**Competing interpretations and classification tension.** Köppen lists the galaxy as non-deficient despite Yoon II morphology; this is expected because strong asymmetry can occur without large integrated deficiency in a mixed tide+RPS event.

**History confidence.** high.

**gFMR/FMR implication — new atlas inference.** Tides first change gas geometry, then weak pressure removes exposed gas; local SFE/metallicity effects can precede a large global H I deficit.

**Specific MAUVE-MUSE/VERTICO test.** Map abundance and SFE separately in the tidal inner arm, pressure-dominated outer arm and tail; compare to the published wind/encounter geometry.

**Adopted mechanism.** old/slow gas depletion. Secondary/contextual process: internal disk evolution.

**H I evidence (direct morphology first).** The H I disk is mildly and symmetrically truncated with no clear current wind feature.

**CO/H2, ionized gas/UV, stars/dust, radio, X-ray and dynamics.** Rich CO/cloud measurements characterize the inner disk but do not supply a dedicated environmental event clock.

**Best-supported event sequence.** A slow process or old weak stripping reduced the outer gas; the present disk is relaxed.

**History confidence.** moderate-low.

**gFMR/FMR implication — new atlas inference.** This is a slow-processing comparison where gas and SFR may evolve together more nearly than in active strippers; resolved cloud physics remains an internal confounder.

## 5.38 NGC4694

### Identity, catalogue properties and adopted status

| Component | Preferred aliases | log Mstar | log SFR | log MHI | log MH2 | DMS |
|---|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| NGC4694 | VCC 2066; UGC 7969; NGC 4694 | 9.92 | -0.87 | 8.43 | 8.09 | -0.71 |

| Component | v (km/s) | MAUVE stage | Yoon | Köppen |
|---|---:|---|---:|---|
| NGC4694 | 1211 | near-peak | II | past |

### NGC4694: observational record and environmental sequence

**Adopted mechanism.** tidal accretion/merger debris. Secondary/contextual process: possible secondary ICM reshaping.

**H I evidence (direct morphology first).** A coherent 38-50 kpc, roughly 1.1e9 Msun H I bridge/debris complex connects NGC4694 with the tidal-dwarf candidate VCC2062; its structure, high column density and stellar bridge favour gravity over a clean wind tail.

**CO/H2, ionized gas/UV, stars/dust, radio, X-ray and dynamics.** CO, young stars and 12+log(O/H) about 8.6-8.7 in VCC2062 prove recycled, pre-enriched tidal material. Molecular depletion times are roughly 1-2e10 yr and can reach 6e10 yr locally.

**Best-supported event sequence.** A slow unequal-mass gas-rich merger produced the bridge and tidal dwarf; ram pressure may reshape the debris but is not the primary explanation.

**Published timing and its meaning.** The completed single-nucleus merger is inferred to be several hundred Myr old. CIGALE burst ages are 376+/-99 Myr globally and 313+/-86 Myr in the CO region; neither is a pressure-peak clock.

**Competing interpretations and classification tension.** Yoon II is contaminated by counting a tidal bridge as a one-sided stripping feature, and Yoon notes the within-disk morphology resembles class III. Köppen's analytical past-strip label is weaker than the dedicated tidal evidence.

**History confidence.** very high for tidal history; low for ICM history.

**gFMR/FMR implication — new atlas inference.** VCC2062 is pre-enriched recycled gas, not pristine inflow or ordinary dwarf chemistry. Merger mixing/inflow may affect NGC4694, but its resolved abundance history remains unmeasured.

**Specific MAUVE-MUSE/VERTICO test.** Use MUSE metallicity/kinematic continuity from disk to bridge and combine with CO to distinguish recycled accretion from any stripped component.

(2026), MAUVE-MUSE: when metallicity follows or fights star formation - a mass-dependent inversion in Virgo galaxies.** [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1093/mnras/stag1019); [ADS](https://ui.adsabs.harvard.edu/abs/2026MNRAS.549g1019H/abstract). *Status/access:* peer reviewed; full text or accepted manuscript. *Tracer/method:* MUSE metallicity and Sigma_SFR; resolved HII-region spaxels in 14 MAUVE galaxies. **Locator:** Secs. 3-6; Figs. 3-6. **Paper-level environmental claim:** The residual metallicity-SFR relation changes sign with calibration and within galaxies; NGC4383 is a robust 0.1-0.2 dex low-metallicity outlier and NGC4192 changes internal sign. *Claim type:* observed analysis. *Timing:* current resolved state. *Uncertainty/alternative:* Strong-line calibration, DIG/HII selection and hierarchical sampling dominate interpretation.
- **Köppen et al. (2018), Ram pressure stripping made easy: an analytical approach.** [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1093/mnras/sty1610); [ADS](https://ui.adsabs.harvard.edu/abs/2018MNRAS.479.4367K/abstract); [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/1806.05887). *Status/access:* peer reviewed; full text. *Tracer/method:* H I deficiency and projected cluster context; analytical peak/time-integrated pressure model. **Locator:** Secs. 7.2-7.3; Tables E1-E5. **Paper-level environmental claim:** Analytical classification: past; compare with direct morphology before adoption. *Claim type:* model-derived. *Timing:* The completed single-nucleus merger is inferred to be several hundred Myr old. CIGALE burst ages are 376+/-99 Myr globally and 313+/-86 Myr in the CO region; neither is a pressure-peak clock.. *Uncertainty/alternative:* Peak pressures are uncertain at roughly factor-of-two level; model assumptions can misclassify tidal systems.
- **Lisenfeld et al. (2016), Molecular gas and star formation in the tidal dwarf galaxy VCC 2062.** [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1051/0004-6361/201527887); [ADS](https://ui.adsabs.harvard.edu/abs/2016A&A...590A..92L/abstract); [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/1604.02974). *Status/access:* peer reviewed; full text. *Tracer/method:* CO, UV-optical SED and deep imaging; VCC2062 and NGC4694 bridge. **Locator:** Table 4; Sect. 6.1. **Paper-level environmental claim:** The stellar bridge confirms tidal origin; burst ages are 376+/-99 Myr globally and 313+/-86 Myr over the CO region, with very long molecular depletion times. *Claim type:* observed plus model-derived. *Timing:* several-hundred-Myr tidal event. *Uncertainty/alternative:* SED burst age is not a unique merger pericentre.
- **Yoon et al. (2017), A history of H I stripping in Virgo: a phase-space view of VIVA galaxies.** [DOI](https://doi.org/10.3847/1538-4357/aa6579); [ADS](https://ui.adsabs.harvard.edu/abs/2017ApJ...838...81Y/abstract); [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/1704.00760). *Status/access:* peer reviewed; full text. *Tracer/method:* H I morphology plus projected phase space; VIVA class and projected phase-space comparison. **Locator:** Sec. 2.1; Table 1; Secs. 4-5. **Paper-level environmental claim:** Yoon class II; provides a statistical stripping-stage context, not a unique orbit. *Claim type:* inferred. *Timing:* stage-scale only. *Uncertainty/alternative:* Projected radius and line-of-sight velocity are degenerate with 3-D orbital state.

## 5.39 NGC4698

### Identity, catalogue properties and adopted status

| Component | Preferred aliases | log Mstar | log SFR | log MHI | log MH2 | DMS |
|---|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| NGC4698 | VCC 2070; UGC 7970; NGC 4698 | 10.47 | -0.85 | 9.24 | 7.81 | -1.05 |

| Component | v (km/s) | MAUVE stage | Yoon | Köppen |
|---|---:|---|---:|---|
| NGC4698 | 1032 | post-peak | I | past |

### NGC4698: observational record and environmental sequence

**Adopted mechanism.** ancient external accretion plus uncertain weak cluster stripping. Secondary/contextual process: possible M49-substructure context.

**H I evidence (direct morphology first).** The H I disk extends to roughly twice the stellar disk and is mostly regular; two short SE tails and NW compression are only tentative weak/incipient RPS indicators, not a classic post-stripping remnant.

**CO/H2, ionized gas/UV, stars/dust, radio, X-ray and dynamics.** Orthogonal bulge/disk and a polar stellar+gas nuclear disk directly prove ancient external acquisition. Its stellar population requires star formation to have ended more than 5 Gyr ago, unrelated to any demonstrated present Virgo event.

**Best-supported event sequence.** An ancient accretion event built the decoupled central structure; a separate incipient, weak or past ICM event remains possible but is not reconstructed by a dedicated modern processing study.

**Published timing and its meaning.** No tailored RPS clock. The polar event is older than 5 Gyr; Köppen's past-strip label is analytical and carries at least factor-of-two pressure uncertainty.

**Competing interpretations and classification tension.** This is the second reclassification audit: Yoon I/VIVA indicate weak or early processing with projection ambiguity, while Köppen labels it past. MAUVE retains post-peak as a sample code, but confidence in that ICM chronology is low; Corsini supports ancient accretion, not post-peak RPS or proven preprocessing.

**History confidence.** high for ancient accretion; low for adopted post-peak ICM status.

**gFMR/FMR implication — new atlas inference.** The ancient polar component may cause central chemical/kinematic decoupling but is not evidence for current dilution. Very low SFR and uncertain gas processing make either FMR projection non-unique.

**Specific MAUVE-MUSE/VERTICO test.** Date separate stellar components, map metallicity/kinematics of the extended H I-linked disk, and test M49-substructure membership with distances rather than projected phase space alone.

### Evidence coverage by tracer

Grades: 0=no material source; 1=contextual; 2=survey/resolved constraint; 3=direct/tailored strong constraint.

|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|

# 6. Cross-sample synthesis

## 6.1 Stage--mechanism conflicts

The three-stage sequence performs best for clean RPS cases with matched morphology and clocks. It performs poorly when stellar gravity dominates (NGC4216, NGC4567/4568, NGC4694), when a merger precedes stripping (NGC4424), when feedback generates extraplanar emission (NGC4064, NGC4383, NGC4569), or when projected phase space conflicts with the gas disk (NGC4192, NGC4698). These are physical exceptions, not bookkeeping failures to erase.

## 6.2 Mixed pathways and subgroup context

Virgo's dynamical youth permits group infall and locally enhanced ICM pressure. Yet preprocessing should be asserted only when companion/group evidence exists. NGC4254 and NGC4654 have strong compound interaction+RPS cases; NGC4216 has direct satellite disruption but an uncertain relationship to H I deficiency; NGC4698 has possible M49 context but no secure current-processing reconstruction.

## 6.3 Timing synthesis

Resolved outer-disk stellar populations commonly date rapid quenching to about 0.1--0.5 Gyr, while tailored models place selected galaxies on either side of peak pressure. Merger/accretion clocks can be hundreds of Myr or >5 Gyr. Tail survival and current radio morphology add still different response times. The atlas therefore stores event-specific clocks instead of one universal `time since infall`.

## 6.4 Implications for resolved metallicity--SFR work

A robust MAUVE chemical analysis should stratify by mechanism and excitation, not only stage. H II-region metallicities require DIG/shock/AGN masks; leading/trailing and inside/outside-truncation comparisons should be reported; pair-overlap spaxels require two-component kinematics; gas mass should be separated into H I and H2; and within-galaxy trends should not be treated as independent population points. NGC4383 is a high-priority low-metallicity outflow/inflow case, while NGC4192's internal sign reversal makes it a calibration and geometry stress test.

## 6.5 Descriptive mechanism counts

| Atlas mechanism group | Components |
|---|---:|
| RPS-dominated | 21 |
| mixed RPS + gravity | 4 |
| gravity/accretion | 4 |
| slow/old depletion | 6 |
| feedback/outflow | 1 |
| uncertain/weak | 4 |

These counts are heuristic summaries of the 40 selected components, not inferred Virgo population fractions.

# 7. Prioritized follow-up programme

1. **Excitation-safe metallicity atlas:** build consistent H II/DIG/shock/AGN masks, repeat multiple strong-line calibrations, and publish leading/trailing plus inside/outside-truncation residuals.
2. **Joint gas-phase accounting:** combine VIVA H I and matched-resolution VERTICO CO to test whether metallicity residuals follow total gas, molecular fraction, SFE or removal geometry.
3. **Event-specific SFHs:** fit outer-disk and leading/trailing stellar populations for survey-only galaxies, explicitly separating quenching time from peak-pressure time.
4. **Compound-history modelling:** prioritize NGC4216, NGC4254, NGC4424, NGC4567/4568, NGC4654, NGC4694 and NGC4698 with stellar+gas dynamics; do not fit an ICM-only model to a gravitationally disturbed disk.
5. **Radio/X-ray persistence tests:** use spectral aging, polarization and hot-gas morphology to determine whether apparently post-peak disks retain active pressure signatures.
6. **Distance/substructure audit:** combine surface-brightness-fluctuation/Tully--Fisher distances and group velocities before assigning M49/M86 preprocessing or backsplash histories.
7. **Pair-specific NGC4567/4568 products:** publish component-deblended mass/SFR/metallicity and a separately masked overlap-front analysis.

## Appendix E. Access gaps and negative results

- The SIMBAD all-mentions inventory is complete only for links returned on 2026-08-12; database indexing and object association are outside this atlas's control.
- A NED object identity query succeeded, but its reference-table endpoint stalled during this execution. NED was not silently treated as returning zero references.
- The ESO Messenger DOI did not resolve through Crossref (HTTP 404) but was checked against the publisher/PDF record.
- Several versions of record were gated; green-open-access manuscripts/preprints supplied full text. Where only metadata or abstract-level access was available, the record is labelled and not used for detailed claims.
- No dedicated modern environmental-processing paper was found for several survey-only galaxies; those chapters explicitly say so.
- Wang et al. 2026, arXiv:2608.04784, is a v1 preprint and cannot outweigh peer-reviewed empirical work.

## Appendix F. Reproducibility and figure provenance

Derived phase-space values are [phase_space_derived_values.csv](/Users/Igniz/Desktop/ICRAR/MAUVE/assets/20260812_mauve_environmental_atlas/phase_space_derived_values.csv). Figure constants, palettes and caveats are [figure_provenance.json](/Users/Igniz/Desktop/ICRAR/MAUVE/assets/20260812_mauve_environmental_atlas/figure_provenance.json). Timeline inputs are [timeline_model_peak.csv](/Users/Igniz/Desktop/ICRAR/MAUVE/assets/20260812_mauve_environmental_atlas/timeline_model_peak.csv) and [timeline_outer_quench.csv](/Users/Igniz/Desktop/ICRAR/MAUVE/assets/20260812_mauve_environmental_atlas/timeline_outer_quench.csv). Mechanism-stage counts are [mechanism_stage_counts.csv](/Users/Igniz/Desktop/ICRAR/MAUVE/assets/20260812_mauve_environmental_atlas/mechanism_stage_counts.csv).

## Appendix G. Citation and claim-integrity statement

No opaque browsing tokens or invented source identifiers are used. Detailed claims are tied to DOI/ADS/arXiv records and source locators. Model-derived histories remain labelled as such; phase-space categories are never promoted to unique orbits. The two reclassification audits and all access limitations remain visible rather than reconciled away.
